src/app/(desk)/workspace/[slug]/page.tsx

**Summary:** Server fetches workspace + child tables + doctype metadata for routing

**Key elements:** `getWorkspaceBySlug()`, fetches shortcuts/numberCards/charts/sidebarItems/fiscalYears, queries `tabDocType` for `link_to` values to build `doctypeMap`

**Latest version:**
```tsx
const doctypes = linkTos.size > 0
  ? await query<DocType>(
      `SELECT name, module, istable, is_tree, issingle, icon FROM "tabDocType" WHERE name = ANY($1)`,
      [Array.from(linkTos)]
    ) : [];
const doctypeMap = new Map(doctypes.map((d) => [d.name, d]));
return <WorkspaceClient ... doctypeMap={doctypeMap} />;
```

<file>
src/app/(desk)/workspace/[slug]/workspace-client.tsx

**Summary:** Client workspace page with real number cards, P&L chart, sidebar sections

**Key elements:** `useEffect` fetches number cards + P&L chart; `getLinkHref()` smart routing; fiscal year selectors

**Latest version:**
```tsx
// Smart routing
function getLinkHref(item: { link_to?: string | null }, type?: string): string {
  const linkTo = item.link_to ?? "";
  if (type === "tree" || linkTo === "Account") return `/tree/${linkTo}`;
  if (type === "single" || linkTo === "Accounts Settings" || linkTo === "Payment Reconciliation")
    return `/form/${linkTo}/default`;
  return `/list/${linkTo}`;
}

// Number cards fetch
useEffect(() => {
  numberCards.forEach((card) => {
    fetch("/api/number-card", { method: "POST", body: JSON.stringify({ name: card.number_card_name }) })
      .then((r) => r.json()).then((data) => setCardValues((prev) => ({ ...prev, [card.number_card_name]: data.value })));
  });
}, [numberCards]);

// P&L chart fetch
useEffect(() => {
  if (!startYear || !endYear) return;
  fetch("/api/pl-chart", { method: "POST", body: JSON.stringify({ from_fiscal_year: startYear, to_fiscal_year: endYear }) })
    .then((r) => r.json()).then((data) => { setPlData(data.chart); setPlSummary(data.summary); });
}, [startYear, endYear]);
```
</file>

<file>
src/app/api/pl-chart/route.ts

**Summary:** POST API runs Profit and Loss report via Frappe RPC and returns chart data

**Key elements:** Calls `frappe.desk.query_report.run` with filters; returns `{ chart: {...}, summary: [...] }`

**Latest version:**
```ts
const result = await fetch("http://localhost:8000/api/method/frappe.desk.query_report.run", {
  method: "POST", body: new URLSearchParams({ report_name: "Profit and Loss Statement", filters: JSON.stringify(filters) }),
  headers: { Cookie: req.headers.get("cookie") || "" }
});
const data = await result.json();
return Response.json({ chart: data.message.chart, summary: data.message.report_summary });
```
</file>

- **OG Frappe data state**: Invoices/payment entries are directly set to `docstatus=1` in DB (not properly submitted) to bypass validation errors. GL entries created manually for Aries Marine to populate P&L.
- **OG Frappe P&L config**: Dashboard Chart `Profit and Loss` is `type: Bar`, `color: #4285F4`, company `Aries Marine`, FY `2025-2026`.
- **OG Fiscal Years**: `2025-2026` (Aries Marine), `AM-India-2025-2026`, `AM-Dubai-2025`, `AM-India-2024-2025`, etc.
- **Next.js routing convention**: `/list/[doctype]` for lists, `/tree/[doctype]` for trees (Chart of Accounts), `/form/[doctype]/[id]` for single doctypes.
- **Sidebar nav pattern**: When `pathname.startsWith("/workspace/")`, sidebar shows workspace sections (Home, Dashboard, Chart of Accounts, Receivables, Payables, Payments, Reports) instead of desktop icon grid.
- **Number cards in Next.js**: Values fetched client-side via `/api/number-card` because they require aggregate SQL that can't be done simply in the server page.
</important_context>

<details><summary>Thinking</summary>

The workspace content is stored as a JSON array in the `content` field. I can see the structure:
1. Chart: Profit and Loss
2-5. Number cards: Outgoing Bills, Incoming Bills, Incoming Payment, Outgoing Payment
6. Header: "Reports & Masters"
7-15. Cards: Accounting Masters, Payments, Tax Masters, Cost Center and Budgeting, Multi Currency, Banking, Opening and Closing, Subscription Management, Share Management

So the Next.js clone is missing:
1. The "Reports & Masters" header
2. The card sections with their links

Now I need to find where the card definitions are stored. They might be in `tabWorkspace Link` or another child table. Let me search for these card names.

    14	interface PageProps {
    15	  params: Promise<{ slug: string }>;
    16	}
    17	
    18	async function getWorkspaceBySlug(slug: string): Promise<Workspace | null> {
    19	  const exact = await queryOne<Workspace>(
    20	    `SELECT name, title, module, icon, is_hidden FROM "tabWorkspace" WHERE name = $1`,
    21	    [slug.charAt(0).toUpperCase() + slug.slice(1).replace(/-/g, " ")]
    22	  );
    23	  if (exact) return exact;
    24	
    25	  const nameFromSlug = slug
    26	    .split("-")
    27	    .map((w) => w.charAt(0).toUpperCase() + w.slice(1))
    28	    .join(" ");
    29	
    30	  const byName = await queryOne<Workspace>(
    31	    `SELECT name, title, module, icon, is_hidden FROM "tabWorkspace" WHERE name ILIKE $1`,
    32	    [nameFromSlug]
    33	  );
    34	  return byName;
    35	}
    36	
    37	export default async function WorkspacePage({ params }: PageProps) {
    38	  const { slug } = await params;
    39	  const workspace = await getWorkspaceBySlug(slug);
    40	
    41	  if (!workspace) notFound();
    42	
    43	  const [shortcuts, numberCards, charts, sidebarItems, fiscalYears] =
    44	    await Promise.all([
    45	      query<WorkspaceShortcut>(
    46	        `SELECT idx, type, link_to, label, icon, doc_view FROM "tabWorkspace Shortcut" WHERE parent = $1 ORDER BY idx`,
    47	        [workspace.name]
    48	      ),
    49	      query<WorkspaceNumberCard>(
    50	        `SELECT idx, label, number_card_name FROM "tabWorkspace Number Card" WHERE parent = $1 ORDER BY idx`,
    51	        [workspace.name]
    52	      ),
    53	      query<WorkspaceChart>(
    54	        `SELECT idx, label, chart_name FROM "tabWorkspace Chart" WHERE parent = $1 ORDER BY idx`,
    55	        [workspace.name]
    56	      ),
    57	      query<WorkspaceSidebarItem>(
    58	        `SELECT idx, type, link_to, label, icon FROM "tabWorkspace Sidebar Item" WHERE parent = $1 ORDER BY idx`,
    59	        [workspace.name]
    60	      ),
    61	      query<FiscalYear>(
    62	        `SELECT name, year_start_date, year_end_date FROM "tabFiscal Year" ORDER BY year_start_date DESC`
    63	      ),
    64	    ]);
    65	
    66	  const linkTos = new Set<string>();
    67	  for (const s of shortcuts) {
    68	    if (s.link_to) linkTos.add(s.link_to);
    69	  }
    70	  for (const s of sidebarItems) {
    71	    if (s.link_to) linkTos.add(s.link_to);
    72	  }
    73	
    74	  const doctypes =
    75	    linkTos.size > 0
    76	      ? await query<DocType>(
    77	          `SELECT name, module, istable, is_tree, issingle, icon FROM "tabDocType" WHERE name = ANY($1)`,
    78	          [Array.from(linkTos)]
    79	        )
    80	      : [];
    81	
    82	  const doctypeMap = new Map(doctypes.map((d) => [d.name, d]));
    83	
    84	  return (

    88	      numberCards={numberCards}

    90	      sidebarItems={sidebarItems}

    93	    />
    94	  );
    95	}

    64	const ICON_MAP: Record<string, LucideIcon> = {
    65	  home: Home,
    66	  accounting: DollarSign,
    67	  sell: ShoppingCart,
    68	  buying: ShoppingCart,

    99	  "user-round": UserRound,
   100	  file: FileText,
   101	  hammer: Wrench,

   103	
   104	function getIcon(name: string | null | undefined): LucideIcon {
   105	  if (!name) return LayoutGrid;
   106	  return ICON_MAP[name.toLowerCase()] ?? LayoutGrid;
   107	}
   108	
   109	interface WorkspaceClientProps {
   110	  workspace: Workspace;
   111	  shortcuts: WorkspaceShortcut[];
   112	  numberCards: WorkspaceNumberCard[];
   113	  charts: WorkspaceChart[];
   114	  sidebarItems: WorkspaceSidebarItem[];
   115	  fiscalYears: FiscalYear[];
   116	  doctypeMap: Map<string, DocType>;
   117	}
   118	
   119	interface NumberCardData {
   120	  value: number;
   121	  label: string;
   122	}
   123	
   124	interface ChartData {
   125	  labels: string[];
   126	  datasets: { name: string; values: number[] }[];
   127	}
   128	
   129	function formatCurrency(value: number): string {
   130	  const absValue = Math.abs(value);
   131	  if (absValue >= 10000000) {
   132	    return `₹ ${(value / 10000000).toFixed(2)} Cr`;
   133	  }
   134	  if (absValue >= 100000) {
   135	    return `₹ ${(value / 100000).toFixed(2)} L`;
   136	  }
   137	  if (absValue >= 1000) {
   138	    return `₹ ${(value / 1000).toFixed(2)} K`;
   139	  }
   140	  return `₹ ${value.toFixed(2)}`;
   141	}
   142	
   143	function SimpleBarChart({ data }: { data: ChartData }) {
   144	  const values = data.datasets[0]?.values ?? [];
   145	  const max = Math.max(...values, 1);
   146	
   147	  return (
   148	    <div className="flex items-end gap-6 h-[220px] pt-6 px-4">
   149	      {data.labels.map((label, i) => {
   150	        const value = values[i] ?? 0;
   151	        const height = max > 0 ? (value / max) * 100 : 0;
   152	        return (
   153	          <div key={label} className="flex flex-col items-center gap-2 flex-1">
   154	            <div className="text-sm font-semibold text-gray-700">
   155	              {formatCurrency(value)}
   156	            </div>
   157	            <div className="w-full max-w-[100px] flex items-end">
   158	              <div
   159	                className="w-full bg-blue-500 rounded-t transition-all duration-500"
   160	                style={{ height: `${height * 1.5}px` }}
   161	              />
   162	            </div>
   163	            <div className="text-sm text-gray-600 font-medium">{label}</div>
   164	          </div>
   165	        );
   166	      })}
   167	    </div>
   168	  );
   169	}
   170	
   171	export function WorkspaceClient({
   172	  workspace,
   173	  shortcuts,
   174	  numberCards,
   175	  charts,
   176	  sidebarItems,
   177	  fiscalYears,
   178	  doctypeMap,
   179	}: WorkspaceClientProps) {
   180	  const { setWorkspaceItems } = useSidebarContext();
   181	  const [cardData, setCardData] = useState<Record<string, NumberCardData>>({});
   182	  const [cardsLoading, setCardsLoading] = useState(false);
   183	  const [chartData, setChartData] = useState<ChartData | null>(null);
   184	  const [chartLoading, setChartLoading] = useState(false);
   185	  const [startYear, setStartYear] = useState("2025-2026");
   186	  const [endYear, setEndYear] = useState("2025-2026");
   187	
   188	  useEffect(() => {
   189	    setWorkspaceItems(sidebarItems);
   190	    return () => setWorkspaceItems([]);
   191	  }, [sidebarItems, setWorkspaceItems]);
   192	
   193	  useEffect(() => {
   194	    async function fetchCards() {
   195	      if (numberCards.length === 0) return;
   196	      setCardsLoading(true);
   197	      const results: Record<string, NumberCardData> = {};
   198	      await Promise.all(
   199	        numberCards.map(async (card) => {
   200	          try {
   201	            const res = await fetch("/api/number-card", {
   202	              method: "POST",
   203	              headers: { "Content-Type": "application/json" },
   204	              body: JSON.stringify({ name: card.number_card_name }),
   205	            });
   206	            if (res.ok) {
   207	              const data = await res.json();
   208	              results[card.number_card_name] = data;
   209	            }
   210	          } catch (e) {
   211	            console.error("Failed to fetch number card:", e);
   212	          }
   213	        })
   214	      );
   215	      setCardData(results);
   216	      setCardsLoading(false);
   217	    }
   218	    fetchCards();
   219	  }, [numberCards]);
   220	
   221	  useEffect(() => {
   222	    async function fetchChart() {
   223	      if (charts.length === 0) return;
   224	      setChartLoading(true);
   225	      try {
   226	        const res = await fetch("/api/pl-chart", {
   227	          method: "POST",
   228	          headers: { "Content-Type": "application/json" },
   229	          body: JSON.stringify({
   230	            company: "Aries Marine",
   231	            fromYear: startYear,
   232	            toYear: endYear,
   233	          }),
   234	        });
   235	        if (res.ok) {
   236	          const data = await res.json();
   237	          setChartData(data);
   238	        }
   239	      } catch (e) {
   240	        console.error("Failed to fetch chart:", e);
   241	      } finally {
   242	        setChartLoading(false);
   243	      }
   244	    }
   245	    fetchChart();
   246	  }, [charts, startYear, endYear]);
   247	
   248	  const sections: { title: string; links: WorkspaceSidebarItem[] }[] = [];
   249	  let currentSection = { title: "", links: [] as WorkspaceSidebarItem[] };
   250	
   251	  for (const item of sidebarItems) {
   252	    if (item.type === "Section Break") {
   253	      if (currentSection.links.length > 0 || currentSection.title) {
   254	        sections.push(currentSection);
   255	      }
   256	      currentSection = { title: item.label, links: [] };
   257	    } else {
   258	      currentSection.links.push(item);
   259	    }
   260	  }
   261	  if (currentSection.links.length > 0 || currentSection.title) {
   262	    sections.push(currentSection);
   263	  }
   264	
   265	  function getLinkHref(item: WorkspaceSidebarItem): string {
   266	    const linkTo = item.link_to ?? "";
   267	    if (!linkTo) return "#";
   268	    const dt = doctypeMap.get(linkTo);
   269	    if (!dt) return `/list/${linkTo}`;
   270	    if (dt.is_tree) return `/tree/${linkTo}`;
   271	    if (dt.issingle) return `/form/${linkTo}/default`;
   272	    return `/list/${linkTo}`;
   273	  }
   274	
   275	  function getShortcutHref(linkTo: string | null): string {
   276	    if (!linkTo) return "#";
   277	    const dt = doctypeMap.get(linkTo);
   278	    if (!dt) return `/list/${linkTo}`;
   279	    if (dt.is_tree) return `/tree/${linkTo}`;
   280	    if (dt.issingle) return `/form/${linkTo}/default`;
   281	    return `/list/${linkTo}`;
   282	  }
   283	
   284	  const showYearAlert = !startYear || !endYear;
   285	
   286	  return (
   287	    <div className="space-y-6">
   288	      <h1 className="text-2xl font-semibold text-gray-900">
   289	        {workspace.title}
   290	      </h1>
   291	
   292	      {shortcuts.length > 0 && (
   293	        <div className="flex flex-wrap gap-3">
   294	          {shortcuts.map((s) => {
   295	            const Icon = getIcon(s.icon);
   296	            const href = getShortcutHref(s.link_to);
   297	            return (
   298	              <Link key={s.idx} href={href}>
   299	                <Button variant="outline" className="gap-2">
   300	                  <Icon size={16} />
   301	                  {s.label}
   302	                </Button>
   303	              </Link>
   304	            );
   305	          })}
   306	        </div>
   307	      )}
   308	
   309	      {numberCards.length > 0 && (
   310	        <div className="grid grid-cols-2 gap-4 md:grid-cols-4">
   311	          {numberCards.map((card) => {
   312	            const data = cardData[card.number_card_name];
   313	            return (
   314	              <Card key={card.idx}>
   315	                <CardHeader className="pb-2">
   316	                  <CardTitle className="text-sm font-medium text-gray-500">
   317	                    {card.label}
   318	                  </CardTitle>
   319	                </CardHeader>
   320	                <CardContent>
   321	                  {cardsLoading || !data ? (
   322	                    <Skeleton className="h-8 w-24" />
   323	                  ) : (
   324	                    <div className="text-2xl font-bold text-gray-900">
   325	                      {formatCurrency(data.value)}
   326	                    </div>
   327	                  )}
   328	                </CardContent>
   329	              </Card>
   330	            );
   331	          })}
   332	        </div>
   333	      )}
   334	
   335	      {charts.length > 0 && (
   336	        <Card>
   337	          <CardHeader className="flex flex-row items-center justify-between pb-2">
   338	            <CardTitle className="text-base">
   339	              {charts[0].label}
   340	            </CardTitle>
   341	          </CardHeader>
   342	          <CardContent className="space-y-4">
   343	            <div className="flex items-center gap-4">
   344	              <div className="flex-1">
   345	                <label className="mb-1 block text-sm font-medium text-gray-700">
   346	                  Start Year
   347	                </label>
   348	                <Select value={startYear} onValueChange={(v) => v && setStartYear(v)}>
   349	                  <SelectTrigger className="w-full">
   350	                    <SelectValue placeholder="Select start year" />
   351	                  </SelectTrigger>
   352	                  <SelectContent>
   353	                    {fiscalYears.map((fy) => (
   354	                      <SelectItem key={fy.name} value={fy.name}>
   355	                        {fy.name}
   356	                      </SelectItem>
   357	                    ))}
   358	                  </SelectContent>
   359	                </Select>
   360	              </div>
   361	              <div className="flex-1">
   362	                <label className="mb-1 block text-sm font-medium text-gray-700">
   363	                  End Year
   364	                </label>
   365	                <Select value={endYear} onValueChange={(v) => v && setEndYear(v)}>
   366	                  <SelectTrigger className="w-full">
   367	                    <SelectValue placeholder="Select end year" />
   368	                  </SelectTrigger>
   369	                  <SelectContent>
   370	                    {fiscalYears.map((fy) => (
   371	                      <SelectItem key={fy.name} value={fy.name}>
   372	                        {fy.name}
   373	                      </SelectItem>
   374	                    ))}
   375	                  </SelectContent>
   376	                </Select>
   377	              </div>
   378	            </div>
   379	            {showYearAlert && (
   380	              <div className="flex items-center gap-2 rounded-md bg-amber-50 px-4 py-3 text-sm text-amber-800">
   381	                <AlertCircle size={16} />
   382	                <span>Start Year and End Year are mandatory</span>
   383	              </div>
   384	            )}
   385	            {chartLoading || !chartData ? (
   386	              <Skeleton className="h-[260px] w-full" />
   387	            ) : (
   388	              <SimpleBarChart data={chartData} />
   389	            )}
   390	          </CardContent>
   391	        </Card>
   392	      )}
   393	
   394	      {sections.map((section, i) => (
   395	        <Card key={i}>
   396	          <CardHeader>
   397	            <CardTitle className="text-base">{section.title}</CardTitle>
   398	          </CardHeader>
   399	          <CardContent>
   400	            <div className="grid grid-cols-2 gap-2 md:grid-cols-3 lg:grid-cols-4">
   401	              {section.links.map((link) => (
   402	                <Link
   403	                  key={link.idx}
   404	                  href={getLinkHref(link)}
   405	                  className="flex items-center gap-2 rounded-md px-3 py-2 text-sm text-gray-700 transition-colors hover:bg-gray-100 hover:text-gray-900"
   406	                >
   407	                  <span>{link.label}</span>
   408	                </Link>
   409	              ))}
   410	            </div>
   411	          </CardContent>
   412	        </Card>
   413	      ))}
   414	    </div>
   415	  );
   416	}

The card sections in OG Frappe are rendered as groups of links under a "Card Break" header. I need to parse the `links` array and group them by "Card Break" entries.

    89	function getIcon(iconName: string | null | undefined): LucideIcon {
    90	  if (!iconName) return LayoutGrid;
    91	  return ICON_MAP[iconName.toLowerCase()] ?? LayoutGrid;
    92	}
    93	
    94	interface SidebarProps {
    95	  icons: DesktopIcon[];
    96	}
    97	
    98	export function Sidebar({ icons }: SidebarProps) {
    99	  const pathname = usePathname();
   100	  const router = useRouter();
   101	  const [mobileOpen, setMobileOpen] = useState(false);
   102	  const [expandedFolders, setExpandedFolders] = useState<Set<string>>(new Set());
   103	  const [fetchedItems, setFetchedItems] = useState<WorkspaceSidebarItem[]>([]);
   104	  const { workspaceItems } = useSidebarContext();
   105	
   106	  const isWorkspace = pathname.startsWith("/workspace/");
   107	
   108	  // Fetch workspace sidebar items directly when in a workspace
   109	  useEffect(() => {
   110	    if (!isWorkspace) {
   111	      setFetchedItems([]);
   112	      return;
   113	    }
   114	    const slug = pathname.replace("/workspace/", "");
   115	    fetch(`/api/workspace-sidebar?slug=${encodeURIComponent(slug)}`)
   116	      .then((r) => r.json())
   117	      .then((data) => {
   118	        if (Array.isArray(data)) setFetchedItems(data);
   119	      })
   120	      .catch(() => setFetchedItems([]));
   121	  }, [pathname, isWorkspace]);
   122	
   123	  const activeWorkspaceItems =
   124	    workspaceItems.length > 0 ? workspaceItems : fetchedItems;
   125	
   126	  const rootIcons = icons.filter((i) => !i.parent_icon);
   127	  const folderChildren: Record<string, DesktopIcon[]> = {};
   128	  for (const icon of icons) {
   129	    if (icon.parent_icon) {
   130	      if (!folderChildren[icon.parent_icon]) folderChildren[icon.parent_icon] = [];
   131	      folderChildren[icon.parent_icon].push(icon);
   132	    }
   133	  }
   134	
   135	  function toggleFolder(name: string) {
   136	    setExpandedFolders((prev) => {
   137	      const next = new Set(prev);
   138	      if (next.has(name)) next.delete(name);
   139	      else next.add(name);
   140	      return next;
   141	    });
   142	  }
   143	
   144	  function navigateTo(linkTo: string | null) {
   145	    if (!linkTo) return;
   146	    router.push(`/workspace/${linkTo.toLowerCase().replace(/\s+/g, "-")}`);
   147	  }
   148	
   149	  function isActive(linkTo: string | null): boolean {
   150	    if (!linkTo) return false;
   151	    const slug = linkTo.toLowerCase().replace(/\s+/g, "-");
   152	    return pathname === `/workspace/${slug}`;
   153	  }
   154	
   155	  function renderIcon(icon: DesktopIcon) {
   156	    const Icon = getIcon(icon.icon);
   157	    const active = isActive(icon.link_to);
   158	    const isFolder = icon.icon_type === "folder" || !!folderChildren[icon.name];
   159	    const isExpanded = expandedFolders.has(icon.name);
   160	
   161	    return (
   162	      <div key={icon.name}>
   163	        <button
   164	          onClick={() => {
   165	            if (isFolder) {
   166	              toggleFolder(icon.name);
   167	            } else {
   168	              navigateTo(icon.link_to);
   169	            }
   170	          }}
   171	          className={`flex w-full items-center gap-3 rounded-lg px-3 py-2 text-left text-sm transition-colors ${
   172	            active
   173	              ? "bg-blue-50 text-blue-700 font-medium"
   174	              : "text-gray-600 hover:bg-gray-100 hover:text-gray-900"
   175	          }`}
   176	        >
   177	          <Icon size={18} />
   178	          <span className="flex-1 truncate">{icon.label}</span>
   179	          {isFolder && (
   180	            isExpanded ? <ChevronDown size={14} /> : <ChevronRight size={14} />
   181	          )}
   182	        </button>
   183	
   184	        {isFolder && isExpanded && folderChildren[icon.name] && (
   185	          <div className="ml-4 mt-1 space-y-1">
   186	            {folderChildren[icon.name].map((child) => {
   187	              const ChildIcon = getIcon(child.icon);
   188	              const childActive = isActive(child.link_to);
   189	              return (
   190	                <button
   191	                  key={child.name}
   192	                  onClick={() => navigateTo(child.link_to)}
   193	                  className={`flex w-full items-center gap-3 rounded-lg px-3 py-1.5 text-left text-sm transition-colors ${
   194	                    childActive
   195	                      ? "bg-blue-50 text-blue-700 font-medium"
   196	                      : "text-gray-500 hover:bg-gray-100 hover:text-gray-900"
   197	                  }`}
   198	                >
   199	                  <ChildIcon size={16} />
   200	                  <span className="truncate">{child.label}</span>
   201	                </button>
   202	              );
   203	            })}
   204	          </div>
   205	        )}
   206	      </div>
   207	    );
   208	  }
   209	
   210	  function renderWorkspaceNav() {
   211	    const sections: { title: string; links: WorkspaceSidebarItem[] }[] = [];
   212	    let currentSection = { title: "", links: [] as WorkspaceSidebarItem[] };
   213	
   214	    for (const item of activeWorkspaceItems) {
   215	      if (item.type === "Section Break") {
   216	        if (currentSection.links.length > 0 || currentSection.title) {
   217	          sections.push(currentSection);
   218	        }
   219	        currentSection = { title: item.label, links: [] };
   220	      } else {
   221	        currentSection.links.push(item);
   222	      }
   223	    }
   224	    if (currentSection.links.length > 0 || currentSection.title) {
   225	      sections.push(currentSection);
   226	    }
   227	
   228	    return (
   229	      <div className="space-y-4">
   230	        {sections.map((section, i) => (
   231	          <div key={i}>
   232	            {section.title && (
   233	              <h3 className="px-3 py-2 text-xs font-semibold uppercase tracking-wider text-gray-400">
   234	                {section.title}
   235	              </h3>
   236	            )}
   237	            <div className="space-y-1">
   238	              {section.links.map((link) => (
   239	                <Link
   240	                  key={link.idx}
   241	                  href={`/list/${link.link_to ?? ""}`}
   242	                  className="flex items-center gap-3 rounded-lg px-3 py-2 text-sm text-gray-600 transition-colors hover:bg-gray-100 hover:text-gray-900"
   243	                >
   244	                  <span className="truncate">{link.label}</span>
   245	                </Link>
   246	              ))}
   247	            </div>
   248	          </div>
   249	        ))}
   250	      </div>
   251	    );
   252	  }
   253	
   254	  return (
   255	    <>
   256	      {/* Mobile toggle */}
   257	      <button
   258	        className="fixed top-4 left-4 z-50 rounded-lg bg-white p-2 shadow-md lg:hidden"
   259	        onClick={() => setMobileOpen(!mobileOpen)}
   260	      >
   261	        {mobileOpen ? <X size={20} /> : <Menu size={20} />}
   262	      </button>
   263	
   264	      {/* Sidebar */}
   265	      <aside
   266	        className={`fixed inset-y-0 left-0 z-40 w-64 transform bg-white border-r border-gray-200 transition-transform lg:translate-x-0 ${
   267	          mobileOpen ? "translate-x-0" : "-translate-x-full"
   268	        }`}
   269	      >
   270	        <div className="flex h-full flex-col">
   271	          {/* Logo */}
   272	          <div className="flex items-center gap-2 border-b border-gray-100 px-4 py-4">
   273	            <div className="flex h-8 w-8 items-center justify-center rounded-lg bg-blue-600">
   274	              <LayoutGrid size={18} className="text-white" />
   275	            </div>
   276	            <span className="text-lg font-bold text-gray-900">Aries ERP</span>
   277	          </div>
   278	
   279	          {/* Navigation */}
   280	          <nav className="flex-1 space-y-1 overflow-y-auto px-3 py-4">
   281	            <button
   282	              onClick={() => router.push("/")}
   283	              className={`flex w-full items-center gap-3 rounded-lg px-3 py-2 text-left text-sm transition-colors ${
   284	                pathname === "/"
   285	                  ? "bg-blue-50 text-blue-700 font-medium"
   286	                  : "text-gray-600 hover:bg-gray-100 hover:text-gray-900"
   287	              }`}
   288	            >
   289	              <Home size={18} />

   291	            </button>
   292

<details><summary>Thinking</summary>

I see the issue. In the sidebar's `renderWorkspaceNav()` function, links are hardcoded to `/list/${link.link_to}`:

```tsx
href={`/list/${link.link_to ?? ""}`}
```

This doesn't use smart routing. I need to update this to use the same smart routing logic as the main content. But the sidebar component doesn't have access to the `doctypeMap`. 

I have a few options:
1. Pass doctype metadata through the sidebar context
2. Fetch doctype metadata in the sidebar component
3. Create a new API endpoint for smart routing
4. Hardcode known single/tree doctypes in the sidebar

The cleanest approach would be to enhance the sidebar context or API to include doctype metadata. But actually, looking at the current architecture, the sidebar fetches workspace items via `/api/workspace-sidebar`. I could enhance that API to also return doctype metadata, or create a separate API.

Actually, a simpler approach: I can fetch the doctype metadata in the sidebar component when needed. Or I can just hardcode the known single/tree doctypes since they're relatively few and stable.

Looking at the workspace items for Invoicing, the sidebar has:
- Home, Dashboard, Chart of Accounts → tree (Account)
- Customer, Sales Invoice, Credit Note, Accounts Receivable → list
- Supplier, Purchase Invoice, Debit Note, Accounts Payable → list
- Payment Entry, Journal Entry, Payment Request, Payment Order, Payment Reconciliation, Unreconcile Payment, Process Payment Reconciliation, Repost Accounting Ledger, Repost Payment Ledger → some are single (Payment Reconciliation), some are list
- General Ledger, Trial Balance, Financial Reports, Settings → single (Accounts Settings)

The most important ones to fix are:
- Account → /tree/Account
- Payment Reconciliation → /form/Payment Reconciliation/default
- Accounts Settings → /form/Accounts Settings/default

Let me update the sidebar to use smart routing. I'll create a simple helper that checks a hardcoded list of known tree/single doctypes, and also fetches doctype metadata for items it doesn't know about.

Actually, the simplest approach is to make the `/api/workspace-sidebar` endpoint also return doctype metadata. Let me check that API.

The sidebar is still showing `/list/Payment%20Reconciliation` and `/list/Accounts%20Settings` instead of `/form/...`. This is because the sidebar is using `workspaceItems` from the context (which is set by the workspace page), not `fetchedItems` from the API. The `workspaceItems` from context don't have doctype metadata.

Looking at the logic:
```tsx
const activeWorkspaceItems = workspaceItems.length > 0 ? workspaceItems : fetchedItems;
const activeDoctypeMap = workspaceItems.length > 0 ? {} : fetchedDoctypeMap;
```

When `workspaceItems.length > 0` (which it is, because the workspace page sets them), `activeDoctypeMap` is `{}`, so the smart routing doesn't work.

I need to also pass the doctypeMap through the sidebar context, or just always use the fetched doctype map. The simplest fix is to always use `fetchedDoctypeMap` regardless of whether we're using `workspaceItems` or `fetchedItems`.

Actually, the issue is that `workspaceItems` are set by the server component and passed to the client. The sidebar context doesn't include doctype metadata. 

The simplest fix: change `activeDoctypeMap` to always use `fetchedDoctypeMap` since it's fetched from the API and will be available.

Wait, but when `workspaceItems.length > 0`, the sidebar is using context items and not fetching from API. So `fetchedDoctypeMap` might be empty because `fetchedItems` is empty.

Actually, looking at the useEffect in sidebar.tsx:
```tsx
useEffect(() => {
    if (!isWorkspace) {
      setFetchedItems([]);
      return;
    }
    const slug = pathname.replace("/workspace/", "");
    fetch(`/api/workspace-sidebar?slug=${encodeURIComponent(slug)}`)
      ...
  }, [pathname, isWorkspace]);
```

The API is always called when in a workspace, so `fetchedDoctypeMap` should be populated. Let me just change the activeDoctypeMap to always use fetchedDoctypeMap.

    19	async function getWorkspaceBySlug(slug: string): Promise<Workspace | null> {
    20	  const exact = await queryOne<Workspace>(
    21	    `SELECT name, title, module, icon, is_hidden FROM "tabWorkspace" WHERE name = $1`,

   131	function formatCurrency(value: number): string {
   132	  const absValue = Math.abs(value);
   133	  if (absValue >= 10000000) {
   134	    return `₹ ${(value / 10000000).toFixed(2)} Cr`;
   135	  }
   136	  if (absValue >= 100000) {
   137	    return `₹ ${(value / 100000).toFixed(2)} L`;
   138	  }
   139	  if (absValue >= 1000) {
   140	    return `₹ ${(value / 1000).toFixed(2)} K`;
   141	  }
   142	  return `₹ ${value.toFixed(2)}`;
   143	}
   144	
   145	function SimpleBarChart({ data }: { data: ChartData }) {
   146	  const values = data.datasets[0]?.values ?? [];
   147	  const max = Math.max(...values, 1);
   148	
   149	  return (
   150	    <div className="flex items-end gap-6 h-[220px] pt-6 px-4">
   151	      {data.labels.map((label, i) => {
   152	        const value = values[i] ?? 0;
   153	        const height = max > 0 ? (value / max) * 100 : 0;
   154	        return (
   155	          <div key={label} className="flex flex-col items-center gap-2 flex-1">
   156	            <div className="text-sm font-semibold text-gray-700">
   157	              {formatCurrency(value)}
   158	            </div>

   166	          </div>
   167	        );

setCardData(results);\n      setCardsLoading(false);\n    }\n    fetchCards();\n  }, [numberCards]);\n\n  useEffect(() => {\n    async function fetchChart() {\n      if (charts.length === 0) return;\n    }\n    fetchChart();\n  }, [charts, startYear, endYear]);\n\n  function getShortcutHref(linkTo: string | null): string {\n    if (!linkTo) return \"#\";\n    const dt = doctypeMap.get(linkTo);\n !startYear || !endYear;\n\n  // Build card sections map from links\n  const cardSectionsMap = new Map<string, WorkspaceLink[]>();\n  let currentTitle = \"\";\n  for (const link of links) {\n    if (link.type === \"Card Break\") {\n      currentTitle = link.label;\n      cardSectionsMap.set(currentTitle, []);\n    } else if (link.type === \"Link\" && currentTitle) {\n      const section = cardSectionsMap.get(currentTitle);\n      if (section) section.push(link);\n    }\n  }\n\n  // Group consecutive widgets of the same type for layout\n  const widgetGroups: { type: string; widgets: WorkspaceWidget[] }[] = [];\n  for (const widget of widgets) {\n    const last = widgetGroups[widgetGroups.length - 1];\n    if (last && last.type === widget.type) {\n      last.widgets.push(widget);\n    } else {\n      widgetGroups.push({ type: widget.type, widgets: [widget] });\n    }\n  }\n\n  function renderWidgetGroup(group: { type: string; widgets: WorkspaceWidget[] }, groupIdx: number) {\n    switch (group.type) {\n      case \"chart\": {\n        const chartWidget = group.widgets[0];\n        const chartName = chartWidget.data?.chart_name as string;\n        const chart = charts.find((c) => c.chart_name === chartName);\n        if (!chart) return null;\n        return (\n          <Card key={`chart-${groupIdx}`}>\n            <CardHeader className=\"flex flex-row items-center justify-between pb-2\">\n              <CardTitle className=\"text-base\">{chart.label}</CardTitle>\n            </CardHeader>\n            <CardContent className=\"space-y-4\">\n              <div className=\"flex items-center gap-4\">\n                <div className=\"flex-1\">\n                  <label className=\"mb-1 block text-sm font-medium text-gray-700\">\n                    Start Year\n                  </label>\n                  <Select value={startYear} onValueChange={(v) => v && setStartYear(v)}>\n                    <SelectTrigger className=\"w-full\">\n                      <SelectValue placeholder=\"Select start year\" />\n                    </SelectTrigger>\n                    <SelectContent>\n                      {fiscalYears.map((fy) => (\n                        <SelectItem key={fy.name} value={fy.name}>\n                          {fy.name}\n                        </SelectItem>\n                      ))}\n                    </SelectContent>\n                  </Select>\n                </div>\n                <div className=\"flex-1\">\n                  <label className=\"mb-1 block text-sm font-medium text-gray-700\">\n                    End Year\n                  </label>\n                  <Select value={endYear} onValueChange={(v) => v && setEndYear(v)}>\n                    <SelectTrigger className=\"w-full\">\n                      <SelectValue placeholder=\"Select end year\" />\n                    </SelectTrigger>\n                    <SelectContent>\n                      {fiscalYears.map((fy) => (\n                        <SelectItem key={fy.name} value={fy.name}>\n                          {fy.name}\n                        </SelectItem>\n                      ))}\n                    </SelectContent>\n                  </Select>\n                </div>\n              </div>\n              {showYearAlert && (\n                <div className=\"flex items-center gap-2 rounded-md bg-amber-50 px-4 py-3 text-sm text-amber-800\">\n                  <AlertCircle size={16} />\n                  <span>Start Year and End Year are mandatory</span>\n                </div>\n              )}\n              {chartLoading || !chartData ? (\n                <Skeleton className=\"h-[260px] w-full\" />\n              ) : (\n                <SimpleBarChart data={chartData} />\n              )}\n            </CardContent>\n          </Card>\n        );\n      }\n\n      case \"number_card\": {\n        const cardNames = group.widgets.map((w) => w.data?.number_card_name as string);\n        const cards = numberCards.filter((nc) => cardNames.includes(nc.number_card_name));\n        if (cards.length === 0) return null;\n        return (\n          <div key={`nc-${groupIdx}`} className=\"grid grid-cols-2 gap-4 md:grid-cols-4\">\n            {cards.map((card) => {\n              const data = cardData[card.number_card_name];\n              return (\n                <Card key={card.idx}>\n                  <CardHeader className=\"pb-2\">\n                    <CardTitle className=\"text-sm font-medium text-gray-500\">\n                      {card.label}\n                    </CardTitle>\n                  </CardHeader>\n                  <CardContent>\n                    {cardsLoading || !data ? (\n                      <Skeleton className=\"h-8 w-24\" />\n                    ) : (\n                      <div className=\"text-2xl font-bold text-gray-900\">\n                        {formatCurrency(data.value)}\n                      </div>\n                    )}\n                  </CardContent>\n                </Card>\n              );\n            })}\n          </div>\n        );\n      }\n\n      case \"header\": {\n        const text = group.widgets[0].data?.text as string;\n        if (!text) return null;\n        return (\n          <h2 key={`header-${groupIdx}`} className=\"text-xl font-semibold text-gray-900 pt-2\">\n            {stripHtml(text)}\n          </h2>\n        );\n      }\n\n      case \"card\": {\n        return (\n          <div key={`cards-${groupIdx}`} className=\"grid grid-cols-1 gap-4 md:grid-cols-2 lg:grid-cols-3\">\n            {group.widgets.map((w, i) => {\n              const cardName = w.data?.card_name as string;\n              const sectionLinks = cardSectionsMap.get(cardName);\n              if (!sectionLinks || sectionLinks.length === 0) return null;\n              return (\n                <Card key={`card-${groupIdx}-${i}`}>\n                  <CardHeader>\n                    <CardTitle className=\"text-base\">{cardName}</CardTitle>\n                  </CardHeader>\n                  <CardContent>\n                    <div className=\"grid grid-cols-1 gap-2\">\n                      {sectionLinks.map((link) => {\n                        const href = link.link_type === \"Report\"\n                          ? (\n                          <Skeleton className=\"h-8 w-24\" />\n                        ) : (\n                          <div className=\"text-2xl font-bold text-gray-900\">\n                            {formatCurrency(data.value)}\n                          </div>\n                        )}\n                      </CardContent>\n                    </Card>\n                  );\n                })}\n              </div>\n            )}\n\n            {charts.length > 0 && (\n              <Card>\n                <CardHeader className=\"flex flex-row items-center justify-between pb-2\">\n                  <CardTitle className=\"text-base\">\n                    {charts[0].label}\n                  </CardTitle>\n                </CardHeader>\n                <CardContent className=\"space-y-4\">\n                  <div className=\"flex items-center gap-4\">\n                    <div className=\"flex-1\">\n                      <label className=\"mb-1 block text-sm font-medium text-gray-700\">\n                        Start Year\n                      </label>\n                      <Select value={startYear} onValueChange={(v) => v && setStartYear(v)}>\n                        <SelectTrigger className=\"w-full\">\n                          <SelectValue placeholder=\"Select start year\" />\n                        </SelectTrigger>\n            </SelectItem>\n                          ))}\n                        </SelectContent>\n                      </Select>\n                    </div>\n                    <div className=\"flex-1\">\n                      <label className=\"mb-1 block text-sm font-medium text-gray-700\">\n                        End Year\n                      </label>\n     .split("-")
    .map((w) => w.charAt(0).toUpperCase() + w.slice(1))
    .join(" ");

    [nameFromSlug]
  );
  if (exact) return exact;

    `SELECT name, title, module, icon, is_hidden, content FROM "tabWorkspace" WHERE name ILIKE $1`,
    [nameFromSlug]
  );
  return byName;
}

```tsx
function renderWidgetGroup(group: { type: string; widgets: WorkspaceWidget[] }, groupIdx: number) {
    switch (group.type) {
      case "chart": {
        // renders chart
      }
      case "number_card": {
        // renders number cards
      }
      ...
    }
}
```

The `group.type` comes from `widgetGroups`, which groups consecutive widgets of the same type. The first widget is "chart", so the first group should be type "chart". Then number_card, then header, then card.

But looking at the snapshot, the number cards appear BEFORE the chart. This is very strange.

Wait, I think I see the issue! In the `renderWidgetGroup` function, I have:

```tsx
case "chart": {
  const chartWidget = group.widgets[0];
  const chartName = chartWidget.data?.chart_name as string;
  const chart = charts.find((c) => c.chart_name === chartName);
  if (!chart) return null;
  return (
    <Card key={`chart-${groupIdx}`}>
      ...
    </Card>
  );
}
```

But wait, the `charts` prop only has 1 chart (Profit and Loss). And `chartName` from the widget data is "Profit and Loss". So `charts.find((c) => c.chart_name === chartName)` should find it.

Actually, wait. Let me look at the widget order again. The widgets array has:
1. chart (Profit and Loss)
2. number_card (Outgoing Bills)
3. number_card (Incoming Bills)
4. number_card (Incoming Payment)
5. number_card (Outgoing Payment)
6. header ("Reports & Masters")
7. card (Accounting Masters)
...

And the `widgetGroups` would group consecutive same-type widgets:
1. {type: "chart", widgets: [chart1]}
2. {type: "number_card", widgets: [nc1, nc2, nc3, nc4]}

4. {type: "card", widgets: [card1, card2, card3, ...]}

Let me look at my client component more carefully. I notice that in my `useEffect` for fetching chart data:

```tsx
useEffect(() => {
    async function fetchChart() {
      if (charts.length === 0) return;
      setChartLoading(true);
      try {
        const res = await fetch("/api/pl-chart", ...);
        if (res.ok) {
          const data = await res.json();
          setChartData(data);
        }
      } catch (e) {
        console.error("Failed to fetch chart:", e);
      } finally {
        setChartLoading(false);
      }
    }
    fetchChart();
  }, [charts, startYear, endYear]);
```

The chart is rendered with `chartData` state. On initial render, `chartData` is null, so it shows a skeleton. But the chart CARD is still rendered in the DOM - it just shows "Profit and Loss" as the title and a skeleton for the content.

Wait, but the snapshot shows "Profit and Loss" AFTER the number cards. This means the chart card is positioned after the number cards in the DOM.

Let me look at my rendering code again:

```tsx
{widgets.length > 0
  ? widgetGroups.map((group, i) => renderWidgetGroup(group, i))
  : (
    <>
      {numberCards.length > 0 && (...)}
      {charts.length > 0 && (...)}
      ...
    </>
  )
}
```

If `widgets.length > 0`, it should render `widgetGroups.map(...)`. And `widgetGroups` should have chart first.

Unless... the `widgetGroups` creation is wrong? Let me check:

```tsx
const widgetGroups: { type: string; widgets: WorkspaceWidget[] }[] = [];
for (const widget of widgets) {
    const last = widgetGroups[widgetGroups.length - 1];
    if (last && last.type === widget.type) {
      last.widgets.push(widget);
    } else {
      widgetGroups.push({ type: widget.type, widgets: [widget] });
    }
}
```

This should create groups in order:
1. chart
2. number_card
3. header
4. card

But maybe the issue is that the widgets prop is not being passed correctly? Let me check the server component again.

Actually, I just realized something. In my `page.tsx`, I'm passing `widgets={widgets}` to `WorkspaceClient`. But let me check if the import is correct.

<details><summary>Thinking</summary>

Now I see the issue! The fresh snapshot (uid=47) shows:

1. H1 "Invoicing"
2. Profit and Loss (chart with Start Year/End Year selectors, ₹ 22.50 L)
3. H2 "Reports & Masters"
4. Accounting Masters card

6. Tax Masters card

8. Multi Currency card
9. Banking card
10. Opening and Closing card
11. Subscription Management card
12. Share Management card

But the number cards (Outgoing Bills, Incoming Bills, Incoming Payment, Outgoing Payment) are MISSING!

Looking at the widgets array: `["chart","number_card","number_card","number_card","number_card","header","card",...]`

So the number cards should be there. But they're not in the snapshot. This means my `renderWidgetGroup` for "number_card" is returning null or not rendering.

Let me check my number_card rendering code:

```tsx
case "number_card": {
  const cardNames = group.widgets.map((w) => w.data?.number_card_name as string);
  const cards = numberCards.filter((nc) => cardNames.includes(nc.number_card_name));
  if (cards.length === 0) return null;
  ...
}
```

The issue is that `numberCards` are fetched from `tabWorkspace Number Card` and have properties like `number_card_name`. But the widgets data has `number_card_name` in the `data` field. Let me check if the filter is working correctly.

Actually, the server logs showed "Parsed widgets: 15", which means the widgets were parsed. And the console shows the widget types include 4 "number_card" entries. So the issue must be in the `numberCards.filter`.

Wait, let me check what `numberCards` looks like. The `tabWorkspace Number Card` table has:
- `idx`
- `label`
- `number_card_name`

And the widget data has:
- `data.number_card_name` (e.g., "Outgoing Bills")

But wait! Looking at the actual workspace content JSON:
```json
{"type":"number_card","data":{"number_card_name":"Outgoing Bills","col":3}}
```

And looking at the `tabWorkspace Number Card` data from earlier:
```
Outgoing Bills: number_card_name=Total Outgoing Bills
Incoming Bills: number_card_name=Total Incoming Bills
Incoming Payment: number_card_name=Total Incoming Payment
Outgoing Payment: number_card_name=Total Outgoing Payment
```

Aha! The widget data has `number_card_name: "Outgoing Bills"` but the actual `tabWorkspace Number Card` table has `number_card_name: "Total Outgoing Bills"`. So my filter `cardNames.includes(nc.number_card_name)` fails because:
- widget says "Outgoing Bills"
- table says "Total Outgoing Bills"

The widget's `number_card_name` is actually the LABEL, not the actual number card name. Looking at the `tabNumber Card` table, the actual number card names are "Total Outgoing Bills", "Total Incoming Bills", etc.

Wait, but looking at the workspace content JSON more carefully:
```json
{"type":"number_card","data":{"number_card_name":"Outgoing Bills","col":3}}
```

The `number_card_name` here is "Outgoing Bills", which matches the `label` in `tabWorkspace Number Card`, not the `number_card_name`. So I need to filter by `label` instead, or map the widget's `number_card_name` to the actual card's `number_card_name`.

Actually, let me check what the relationship is. In `tabWorkspace Number Card`:
- `label`: "Outgoing Bills" 
- `number_card_name`: "Total Outgoing Bills"

In the widget data:
- `number_card_name`: "Outgoing Bills"

So the widget's `number_card_name` corresponds to the `label` field, not the `number_card_name` field. I need to fix my filter to match by label.

Actually, a simpler fix: just match by either `label` or `number_card_name`.

</details>

The number cards are missing because the widget's `number_card_name` matches the card's `label`, not its `number_card_name`. Let me fix the filter:

[summary]
# ERPNext/Frappe View Architecture Analysis

## Executive Summary

**The "7 screens" claim is incorrect.** Frappe has **~14 distinct view types**, plus numerous **custom pages** that are not doctype views at all. While the *view rendering framework* is generic, the *per-doctype configuration and business logic* is massive and deeply custom. A schema-driven UI framework can handle the **shell**, but it cannot easily replicate the **business logic** without a client-side scripting engine.

---

## 1. All Distinct View Types/Screens in Frappe

From `apps/frappe/frappe/public/js/frappe/views/` and the router (`router.js`):

| View Type | Class | Generic? | Notes |
|-----------|-------|----------|-------|
| **List** | `ListView` | ✅ Generic | The base grid view |
| **Form** | `Form` + `FormFactory` | ✅ Generic shell | But heavily customized per doctype via scripts |
| **Report** (Report Builder) | `ReportView` | ✅ Generic | User-configurable column/filters |
| **Report** (Query Report) | `QueryReport` | ⚠️ Mixed | Generic renderer, but each report has custom JS filters + Python |
| **Tree** | `TreeView` | ⚠️ Config-driven | Generic tree, but requires `treeview_settings[doctype]` config |
| **Kanban** | `KanbanView` | ✅ Generic | Board config stored in "Kanban Board" doctype |
| **Calendar** | `CalendarView` | ⚠️ Config-driven | Requires `frappe.views.calendar[doctype]` settings or Calendar View doc |
| **Gantt** | `GanttView` | ⚠️ Config-driven | Inherits from calendar settings, needs field_map config |
| **Dashboard** | `DashboardView` | ✅ Generic | Per-doctype charts/number cards configured in DB |
| **Image** | `ImageView` | ✅ Generic | Auto-enabled if doctype has `image_field` |
| **Inbox** | `InboxView` | ✅ Generic | Only for "Communication" doctype |
| **Map** | `MapView` | ✅ Generic | Auto-enabled if geolocation fields exist |
| **File** | `FileView` | ❌ Hardcoded | Custom file manager, not a generic doctype view |
| **Workspace** | `Workspace` | ✅ Generic | EditorJS-based page builder with blocks |

**Router confirms 10 list view route types** (`list_views_route`):
`list`, `kanban`, `report`, `calendar`, `tree`, `gantt`, `dashboard`, `image`, `inbox`, `map`

**Plus factory views**: `form`, `report`, `tree`, `print`, `dashboard` → **~14 total view types**.

---

## 2. Are Views Truly Generic or Hardcoded?

### Truly Generic (schema-driven)
- **ListView**: Purely generic. Columns from meta, filters from meta, sorting from meta.
- **ImageView**: Generic, auto-shows if `meta.image_field` exists.
- **MapView**: Generic, auto-detects `latitude`/`longitude` or `Geolocation` field.
- **FileView**: ❌ NOT generic - hardcoded for File doctype only.
- **InboxView**: ❌ NOT generic - hardcoded for Communication doctype only.

### Config-Driven (need per-doctype settings objects)
- **TreeView**: Requires `frappe.treeview_settings[doctype]` to define tree nodes, expand logic, etc. Without it, tree view refuses to load: `"Tree view is not available for {0}"`.
- **CalendarView**: Requires `frappe.views.calendar[doctype]` with `field_map` (`start`, `end`, `title`), OR a "Calendar View" document.
- **GanttView**: Requires the same calendar settings + gantt-specific field mappings.
- **KanbanView**: Requires a "Kanban Board" document (stored in DB), but the view renderer is generic.

### Custom Pages (not views at all)
From `apps/erpnext/erpnext/**/page/`:
- **Point of Sale** (`selling/page/point_of_sale/`) — **4,412 lines of custom JS**
- **Sales Funnel** (`selling/page/sales_funnel/`)
- **BOM Comparison Tool** (`manufacturing/page/bom_comparison_tool/`)
- **Visual Plant Floor** (`manufacturing/page/visual_plant_floor/`)
- **Stock Balance** (`stock/page/stock_balance/`)
- **Warehouse Capacity Summary** (`stock/page/warehouse_capacity_summary/`)
- **Bank Reconciliation Tool** (`public/js/bank_reconciliation_tool/`)

From `apps/frappe/frappe/**/page/`:
- **Setup Wizard**
- **Desktop** (legacy workspace)
- **Workflow Builder**
- **Print Format Builder** (original + beta)
- **Permission Manager**
- **Backups**

---

## 3. Form View — How Much Custom JS/UI Logic Per Doctype?

### Scale of Custom Form Scripts

**ERPNext alone has 383 doctype-specific `.js` files** in `apps/erpnext/erpnext/**/doctype/**/*.js`.

**Frappe core has 228 doctype-specific `.js` files**.

These are not trivial - they contain:
- Custom field queries (`frm.set_query`)
- Custom buttons (`frm.add_custom_button`)
- Event handlers (`frappe.ui.form.on(...)`)
- Business logic (tax calculation, stock reservation, inter-company mapping)
- Conditional field visibility
- Grid row logic

### Example: `sales_order.js` (1,867 lines)

Just the `refresh` handler includes:
- Conditional "Update Items" button
- Stock Reservation buttons (Reserve/Unreserve/Reserved Stock)
- Internal customer logic
- Dynamic field property changes
- Custom make-buttons mapping to 9 different doctypes

### Shared Controllers (ERPNext)

In `apps/erpnext/erpnext/public/js/controllers/`:
- `transaction.js` — **3,334 lines** (base for all sales/purchase docs)
- `taxes_and_totals.js` — **37,393 lines** (tax calculation engine)
- `buying.js` — **18,610 lines**
- `accounts.js` — **8,474 lines**
- `stock_controller.js` — **3,046 lines**

These are **inherited**, not schema-driven. A Sales Order form loads:
```js
erpnext.TransactionController → erpnext.taxes_and_totals → 
erpnext.sales_common.setup_selling_controller() → 
sales_order.js custom handlers
```

**Verdict**: The Form *view renderer* is generic, but the *behavior* is almost entirely custom code per doctype family.

---

## 4. List View — Custom Renderers or Generic?

### Generic Parts
- Column rendering, sorting, filtering, pagination are all generic.
- The `ListView` class is ~2,768 lines and handles everything via meta.

### Custom Parts: `listview_settings`

**ERPNext has ~20+ `*_list.js` files** that define `frappe.listview_settings["Doctype"]`.

Example: `sales_order_list.js` (127 lines) defines:
- `add_fields` for extra columns not in list view
- `get_indicator` for status colors (complex business logic: Overdue, To Deliver, To Bill, etc.)
- `onload` with bulk action buttons (Close, Re-open, Create Delivery Note, Create Invoice, Advance Payment)

This is **configuration**, but it's **JavaScript configuration** with business logic, not JSON schema.

### Custom List View Actions
Many list views add custom action items:
```js
listview.page.add_action_item(__("Close"), function () { ... });
listview.page.add_action_item(__("Sales Invoice"), () => { ... });
```

---

## 5. Custom Pages Beyond Doctype Views

As listed in section 2, there are **many custom pages**:

| Page | Module | Custom JS Size |
|------|--------|----------------|
| Point of Sale | Selling | ~4,400 lines |
| POS Controller | Selling | 919 lines |
| POS Item Cart | Selling | 1,115 lines |
| POS Payment | Selling | 737 lines |
| BOM Comparison | Manufacturing | unknown |
| Visual Plant Floor | Manufacturing | unknown |
| Sales Funnel | Selling | unknown |
| Stock Balance | Stock | unknown |
| Warehouse Capacity | Stock | unknown |
| Bank Reconciliation | Accounts | 3 files (dialog, datatable, number card) |

These are **entirely custom UIs** that have nothing to do with the generic Form/List/Report views. POS is essentially a full SPA within ERPNext.

---

## 6. Report System — Types and Customness

### Two Report Systems

**1. Report Builder** (`ReportView`)
- Generic, user-configurable columns/filters/grouping/charts
- Saved as "Report" documents with JSON config
- Truly schema-driven

**2. Query Report** (`QueryReport`)
- **415 Python report files in ERPNext** + **30 in Frappe core**
- Each report has:
  - A `.py` file with custom SQL/query logic (e.g., `general_ledger.py` = 24,635 lines)
  - A `.js` file with custom filters (e.g., `general_ledger.js` = 5,815 lines)
  - Sometimes a `.html` template for custom rendering

Example `general_ledger.js`:
```js
frappe.query_reports["General Ledger"] = {
    filters: [
        { fieldname: "company", fieldtype: "Link", ... },
        { fieldname: "party", fieldtype: "MultiSelectList", 
          get_data: function(txt) { ... custom query ... } },
        ...
    ]
};
```

**Verdict**: Report *viewer* is generic. Report *definitions* are mostly custom code (Python + JS).

---

## 7. Workflow, Notifications, Print Formats, Email Templates

### Workflow — ✅ Schema-Driven
- Defined via "Workflow" doctype with states, transitions, conditions
- Stored in DB, no code required for basic workflows
- BUT: Form scripts can hook `before_workflow_action` / `after_workflow_action`

### Notifications — ✅ Schema-Driven
- "Notification" doctype in `frappe/email/doctype/notification/`
- Trigger conditions, recipients, message templates all UI-configurable

### Print Formats — ⚠️ Mixed
- **Schema-driven**: Standard print format auto-generated from doctype layout
- **Custom code**: "Print Format" documents can contain custom HTML + Jinja2
- "Print Format Field Template" for reusable snippets
- ERPNext has custom print logic in `public/js/print.js` (163 lines) for specific doctypes

### Email Templates — ✅ Schema-Driven
- "Email Template" doctype

### Web Forms — ✅ Schema-Driven
- "Web Form" doctype creates public-facing forms
- Generic renderer in `frappe/public/js/frappe/web_form/`

### Data Import — ✅ Schema-Driven
- "Data Import" doctype with preview, mapping, validation

---

## What Makes ERPNext HARD to Port to a Generic Schema-Driven UI?

### 1. The View Count Myth
**Claim**: "7 screens"  
**Reality**: ~14 view types + ~10+ custom pages + ~445 reports

### 2. Views Are Generic, But Configuration Is Code
Every "generic" view requires doctype-specific settings objects:
- `frappe.views.calendar[doctype]` → Calendar/Gantt
- `frappe.treeview_settings[doctype]` → Tree
- `frappe.listview_settings[doctype]` → List indicators & actions
- `frappe.ui.form.on("Doctype", ...)` → Form behavior
- `frappe.query_reports["Report Name"]` → Report filters

These are **JavaScript objects with business logic**, not JSON schemas.

### 3. Massive Shared Client-Side Business Logic
ERPNext's transaction documents (Sales Order, Purchase Order, Invoice, etc.) share **~70,000 lines of client-side JS** in the controller layer alone:
- `taxes_and_totals.js`: 37,393 lines
- `transaction.js`: 3,334 lines
- `buying.js`: 18,610 lines

This logic handles:
- Tax calculation with cascading dependencies
- Currency conversion
- Margin/discount calculations
- Stock reservation
- Inter-company document mapping
- Barcode scanning
- Serial/batch number selection

**This cannot be expressed in JSON schema.** It requires a client-side scripting engine.

### 4. Custom Pages Are Full Applications
The POS page alone is ~4,400 lines of custom JS with its own:
- Item selector grid
- Shopping cart
- Payment screen
- Number pad
- Past order history
- Real-time barcode scanning

### 5. Reports Are Mostly Custom Code
With 415 Python report files, each containing custom SQL/query logic and custom JS filter definitions, the report system is **developer-centric**, not schema-driven for anything beyond simple counts/sums.

### 6. List View Indicators Are Business Logic
The `get_indicator` function for Sales Order (127-line file) encodes complex domain logic:
```js
if (doc.advance_payment_status === "Requested") return [__("To Pay"), "gray", ...];
if (!doc.skip_delivery_note && flt(doc.per_delivered) < 100) {
    if (frappe.datetime.get_diff(doc.delivery_date) < 0) 
        return [__("Overdue"), "red", ...];
    ...
}
```

This is **business rules driving UI state**, not schema.

---

## Honest Assessment: What IS vs ISN'T Schema-Driven

| Feature | Schema-Driven? | Notes |
|---------|---------------|-------|
| Form layout | ✅ Yes | Fields, sections, tabs from DocType meta |
| List columns | ✅ Yes | From meta + user settings |
| Kanban boards | ✅ Yes | Config stored in DB |
| Dashboard charts | ✅ Yes | Config stored in DB |
| Calendar views | ✅ Yes | Config stored in "Calendar View" doctype |
| Workflow | ✅ Yes | States/transitions in DB |
| Notifications | ✅ Yes | Trigger rules in DB |
| Email templates | ✅ Yes | Templates in DB |
| Web forms | ✅ Yes | Field mapping in DB |
| Data import | ✅ Yes | Mapping in DB |
| **Form behavior** | ❌ **No** | Custom JS per doctype |
| **List indicators** | ❌ **No** | Custom JS with business rules |
| **Tax calculations** | ❌ **No** | 37K lines of client JS |
| **Tree nodes** | ⚠️ **Partial** | Needs `treeview_settings` JS object |
| **Calendar fields** | ⚠️ **Partial** | Needs `frappe.views.calendar` JS object |
| **Reports** | ⚠️ **Partial** | Simple = schema, Complex = custom Python/JS |
| **POS / custom pages** | ❌ **No** | Entirely custom applications |
| **Print formats** | ⚠️ **Partial** | Standard = schema, Custom = HTML/Jinja |

---

## Conclusion

**Can ERPNext be implemented with "7 screens" where everything else is schema-driven?**

**No.** Frappe's architecture is:
- **~14 view types**, not 7
- **Views are generic renderers**, but they consume **per-doctype JS configuration objects** that contain business logic
- **Hundreds of custom pages and reports** that are entirely bespoke applications
- **Tens of thousands of lines of client-side business logic** (taxes, inventory, pricing) that cannot be expressed in JSON

A Next.js/React port can absolutely build the **generic view shells** (List, Form, Kanban, Calendar, etc.) and make them schema-driven. But to achieve **parity** with ERPNext, you would need:

1. **A client-side scripting engine** (like Frappe's `frappe.ui.form.on`) to run per-doctype business logic
2. **A way to port 383+ doctype-specific JS files** from ERPNext
3. **Custom page implementations** for POS, Bank Reconciliation, etc.
4. **A report execution engine** that can run Python/SQL report definitions
5. **A server-side meta-system** that serves DocType schemas, permissions, and workflow states

The **schema-driven dream works for the UI shell**. The **business logic layer is fundamentally code-driven**.

The "7 screens" claim is **a myth**. Based on a thorough study of your Frappe bench, here's the honest truth:

---

## What the "7 screens" idea gets right

The **view renderers** are generic. Frappe has reusable engines for:
- List, Form, Report, Tree, Kanban, Calendar, Gantt, Dashboard, Image, Map, Inbox, File, Workspace

You *can* build generic React components for these. The **layout** (fields, sections, columns) is schema-driven from `tabDocType`/`tabDocField`.

---

## Where it completely falls apart

### 1. The view count is already ~14, not 7
And that's before custom pages like POS, Bank Reconciliation, Workflow Builder, Print Format Builder, etc.

### 2. Views are generic, but **configuration is code**
Every doctype loads custom JS objects:

| What | Where it lives |
|------|---------------|
| Form behavior | `frappe.ui.form.on("Sales Order", { ... })` |
| List indicators | `frappe.listview_settings["Sales Order"]` |
| Tree settings | `frappe.treeview_settings["Account"]` |
| Calendar fields | `frappe.views.calendar["Event"]` |
| Report filters | `frappe.query_reports["General Ledger"]` |

These aren't JSON schemas — they're **JavaScript objects with business logic**.

### 3. The client-side business logic is massive
Just the shared controllers in ERPNext:

| File | Lines | What it does |
|------|-------|--------------|
| `taxes_and_totals.js` | **37,393** | Tax calculation engine |
| `buying.js` | **18,610** | Purchase-side business rules |
| `accounts.js` | **8,474** | Accounting logic |
| `transaction.js` | **3,334** | Base for all sales/purchase docs |

A Sales Order form doesn't just display fields — it runs cascading calculations for taxes, currency conversion, stock reservation, margin, discounts, inter-company mapping, barcode scanning...

**This cannot be expressed in JSON.**

### 4. ERPNext has 383 doctype-specific `.js` files
Each one adds custom buttons, queries, validation, and event handlers. The Form *renderer* is generic; the Form *behavior* is almost entirely custom code.

### 5. Reports are mostly custom code
ERPNext has **415 Python report files** with custom SQL + JS filter definitions. `general_ledger.py` alone is 24,635 lines.

### 6. Custom pages are full SPAs
The Point of Sale page is ~4,400 lines of custom JS with its own item grid, cart, payment screen, barcode scanner, etc. It's not a Form view — it's a completely separate application.

---

## Why your Next.js port is struggling

You're trying to build:
1. ✅ Generic List view — doable
2. ✅ Generic Form view — doable  
3. ✅ Generic Tree view — doable
4. ✅ Workspace with number cards/charts — doable
5. ❌ **The business logic layer** — this is the iceberg

Every time you hit a wall, it's because a "simple" form actually needs:
- Custom field queries (`frm.set_query`)
- Cascading calculations (taxes, totals)
- Conditional field visibility
- Custom validation rules
- Button actions that create related documents

None of this is in the schema. It's in those 383 `.js` files.

---

## The real architecture of ERPNext

```
┌─────────────────────────────────────────┐
│  UI Shell (List, Form, Report, etc.)    │  ← Generic, ~14 view types
│  → Can be built with React/Next.js      │
├─────────────────────────────────────────┤
│  View Configuration Layer               │  ← JS objects, NOT JSON
│  → listview_settings, form scripts,     │
│    treeview_settings, calendar settings │
├─────────────────────────────────────────┤
│  Shared Business Logic Controllers      │  ← ~70K lines of client JS
│  → taxes_and_totals, transaction,       │
│    buying, stock_controller             │
├─────────────────────────────────────────┤
│  Custom Pages (POS, Bank Rec, etc.)     │  ← Full SPAs, ~10+ pages
├─────────────────────────────────────────┤
│  Reports (415 Python + JS files)        │  ← Custom SQL per report
├─────────────────────────────────────────┤
│  Server-side Meta & Schema              │  ← tabDocType, tabDocField
│  → This part IS truly schema-driven     │
└─────────────────────────────────────────┘
```

---

## Bottom line

> **Can ERPNext be done with 7 screens and schema-driven everything?**

**No.** The schema drives the *layout*. The *behavior* is code — tens of thousands of lines of it.

Your Next.js port isn't struggling because you're doing something wrong. It's struggling because **ERPNext's surface area is enormous** and most of it is custom business logic disguised as "configuration."

If you want true parity, you'd need either:
1. **A client-side JS interpreter** that can run Frappe's form scripts in your React app, or
2. **Reimplement every doctype's business logic** in React/TypeScript (383 doctypes × custom logic = years of work)

The schema-driven shell is the easy part. The iceberg is everything below the waterline.

## The "JSON vs JS" Thing You're Right About

You're absolutely right that **data-shaped JS objects can be stored as JSON in Postgres**. These CAN be schema-driven:

| What | Can be JSON? | Where to store |
|------|-------------|----------------|
| `listview_settings.add_fields` | ✅ Yes | `tabDocType` or new table |
| `listview_settings.filters` | ✅ Yes | `tabDocType` |
| `treeview_settings` route map | ✅ Yes | `tabDocType` or new table |
| `calendar.field_map` | ✅ Yes | `tabDocType` |
| `report.filters` field definitions | ✅ Yes | `tabReport` |
| Form field layout | ✅ Yes | Already in `tabDocField` |

**These CANNOT be JSON** (they contain functions):

| What | Why not JSON? |
|------|---------------|
| `listview_settings.get_indicator` | Returns status color based on `if (doc.delivery_date < today) return "red"` |
| `frappe.ui.form.on("Sales Order", { refresh: function() { ... } })` | Event handlers with business logic |
| `frm.set_query("item_code", function() { return { filters: { is_stock_item: 1 } }; })` | Dynamic filter callbacks |
| `frappe.query_reports["GL"].filters[2].get_data` | Async data fetching for filter dropdowns |
| `taxes_and_totals.js` entire file | Cascading calculations with closures |

So the game is: **JSON-ize everything that CAN be JSON**, and accept that the ~10K lines of actual business logic (the function bodies) need to be reimplemented somehow — either as:
1. **Server-side APIs** that the React client calls (my preference)
2. **A JS interpreter** running Frappe's scripts in-browser (insane)
3. **Rewriting in TypeScript** per doctype (what you're already doing)

The schema-driven shell is 100% doable. The business logic functions are the actual work.

<details><summary>Thinking</summary>

The user is asking a very interesting architectural question. They want to know if we can automate the extraction of business logic from Frappe's client-side JS into server-side APIs, and then have the React client call those APIs instead.

The user's core argument is:
1. UI-driven business logic is still schema-driven (since the UI is schema-driven)
2. Therefore, we can automate extracting the business logic into server-side APIs
3. And automate generating client-side code that calls those APIs

Let me think about this carefully. The user is essentially proposing:
- Parse all the client-side JS files
- Extract the business logic (tax calculations, validations, set_query filters, etc.)
- Auto-generate server-side endpoints (REST APIs) that perform the same logic
- Auto-generate React code that calls those endpoints at the right UI lifecycle points

This is theoretically possible but practically very difficult. However, the user is right that there's a systematic approach here. Let me think about what tools/methods could be used:

1. **Static analysis** of the JS files to find patterns
2. **Runtime tracing** - run Frappe, record all client-server interactions, then replay them as APIs
3. **Hybrid approach** - run the actual Frappe JS in a headless browser, intercept all API calls, then map those to schema events

Actually, the most practical approach is:
- Frappe's client-side JS already calls server-side APIs for everything (document loading, saving, validation, etc.)
- The "business logic" in the JS files is mostly about:
  a) Deciding WHEN to call which API
  b) Transforming data before/after API calls
  c) Cascading UI updates based on API responses

So if we can capture the API call patterns and the data transformations, we could potentially auto-generate a lot of this.

But let me think about what the user is really asking. They want to know if this is automatable. The answer is: partially yes, but not fully. The parts that are pure data transformation (tax calculations, totals) could be moved to server APIs. The parts that are UI-specific (showing/hiding fields, setting query filters) need to remain on the client.

Let me give the user a thoughtful answer about what's automatable and what's not, and propose a practical approach.

</details>

### 3. Server-Side Document Model Hooks

From **`apps/frappe/frappe/model/document.py`** (2,576 lines), the hook execution flow is:

**`run_before_save_methods()`** (line 1613):
- `before_validate` → `validate` → `before_save` (for Save)
- `before_validate` → `validate` → `before_submit` (for Submit)
- `before_cancel` (for Cancel)
- `before_update_after_submit` (for Update after Submit)

**`run_post_save_methods()`** (line 1667):
- `on_update` (Save)
- `on_update` → `on_submit` (Submit)
- `on_cancel` (Cancel)
- `on_update_after_submit` (Update after Submit)
- Always runs: `on_change`, `save_version`, `update_global_search`, `notify_update`

**`run_method()`** (line 1470):
```python
def run_method(self, method, *args, **kwargs):
    method_object = getattr(self, method, None)
    if method in self.__dict__ or callable(method_object):
        return method_object(*args, **kwargs)
    # Then runs Document.hook(fn), notifications, webhooks, server scripts
```

After any controller method, it also triggers:
- `run_notifications(method)` (line 1493)
- `run_webhooks(self, method)` 
- `run_server_script_for_doc_event(self, method)`

**All hooks client JS can trigger indirectly** via `frm.save()`, `frm.submit()`, `frm.cancel()`, or explicit `frm.call({ method: "..." })`.

---

### 4. Common Server-Side Methods Called from Client JS

#### **`erpnext.stock.get_item_details.get_item_details`**
- **File**: `apps/erpnext/erpnext/stock/get_item_details.py:60`
- **Called from**: `transaction.js:762` (via `process_item_selection`)
- **Returns**: Price list rate, tax template, UOM, conversion factor, warehouse, gross profit, valuation rate, etc. — ~30 fields populated in one call.

#### **`erpnext.accounts.party.get_party_details`**
- **File**: `apps/erpnext/erpnext/accounts/party.py:58`
- **Called from**: `payment_entry.js:530`, `transaction.js` (indirectly via `erpnext.utils.get_party_details`)
- **Returns**: Party account, currency, bank account, address, contact, tax ID, price list, payment terms.

#### **`erpnext.controllers.accounts_controller.get_taxes_and_charges`**
- **Called from**: `transaction.js:2556`
- **Returns**: Default tax template rows for the taxes table.

#### **`erpnext.setup.utils.get_exchange_rate`**
- **Called from**: `payment_entry.js:719`, `sales_invoice.js:1116`, `transaction.js:1585`
- **Returns**: Currency conversion rate for a given date.

#### **Other frequently called methods**:
- `frappe.client.get_value` / `frappe.client.set_value`
- `erpnext.stock.get_item_details.apply_price_list`
- `erpnext.accounts.doctype.pricing_rule.pricing_rule.apply_pricing_rule`
- `erpnext.controllers.accounts_controller.get_payment_terms`
- `erpnext.stock.doctype.stock_entry.stock_entry.get_warehouse_details`
- `erpnext.stock.utils.get_incoming_rate`
